#417. Four Ways to Help Newcomers Feel Like They Belong

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Become a subscribing member today. Help us continue to bring “the science of a meaningful life” to you and to millions around the globe.

Greater Good Science Center • Magazine • In Action • In Education

Liz Cahill was born and raised in Philadelphia. Her whole family is from Philadelphia, going back generations. One day, she was offered an internship in the Bay Area and decided to make the leap, despite not knowing anything about it or anyone there.

“I said goodbye to everything I knew and loved in Philadelphia and came to the Bay. Not knowing a soul.”

When she arrived, it hit her. “I was like, wow, I’m in a place that like, literally nobody knows me. So if I am dead in a ditch, like nobody will figure it out for a long time.”

This could have been a story of loneliness and disconnection, but it wasn’t, because someone took her under their wings—actually two strangers, her roommates Lauren and Deirdre. “They took me in and welcomed me and brought me into their whole social world, which showed me so much love.”

This isn’t the experience for most people. Darilys Matos Acevedo grew up in Puerto Rico. “In Puerto Rico, a stranger talks to you all the time. If you went down the street and you talked to somebody, if you made eye contact . . . you had to smile at them or you said good morning or good afternoon.”

Then she moved to Boston for graduate school. “I had never even visited the place. I knew nobody here.” And where she got smiles and small talk in Puerto Rico, in Boston she “got glares.” “And I’m like, oh, OK. You don’t do any of that here.” It was painful and created a large void in her life.

As Darilys talked to other Latinos in Boston, she assumed “I’m the only one who feels this way, but I wasn’t. There was like a whole group of people that felt like that.”

Their stories are different, but they reveal the same truth.

Moving doesn’t simply change where we live. It interrupts the invisible web of relationships that makes everyday life feel familiar, that makes us feel like we belong. And the majority of the 13 million American adults who move each year experience this.

At the US Chamber of Connection, our recent study on social connection in America explored the impact of life transitions on social connection. It found that 40% of people navigating a relocation struggle with social connection, and they report 35% more barriers to connection compared to everyone else. Two of the most common barriers are not knowing where to start and not wanting to go out alone.

The impact is lasting, as someone shared with us. “Two years ago, I was going on a vacation, and I could not find ONE neighbor to water my plants while I was away. I had lived in this building on and off for over 15 years. I was mortified. How could it be that I had nobody in my building to help with such a small task?”

Moving ranks among the most stressful life events a person can experience, on par with divorce and job loss. But the stress isn’t just logistical; it’s psychological. Moving severs the informal ties and daily routines that quietly anchor our sense of who we are and where we belong. The result is a predictable but rarely named experience: not just loneliness, but identity dislocation. The question isn’t only “who do I know here?” but “who am I here?”

This month, we're teaming up with the US Chamber of Connection for Welcome Week. Together, we're mobilizing communities, neighborhoods, and organizations nationwide to do one simple thing this September: open the door to a new friend. Make a pledge to invite someone in and help us reach our goal to make 100,000 people feel welcomed!

To understand the scale of that loss, consider what a typical adult social network requires to build: roughly 200 hours to develop a close friend, 90 hours for a friend, and five hours of repeated interaction for each of the weak ties (neighbors, familiar faces, community contacts) that make daily life feel anchored. A typical adult carries around 1,300 hours of accumulated relationship time. When you move, you leave all of it behind and start from zero.

This is also, paradoxically, what makes the first year after a move one of the most powerful windows for connection. Disruption breaks old habits and makes people unusually open to forming new ones, a phenomenon researchers call the habit discontinuity effect. People who have just moved are more likely to join things, try things, and reach out than they were six months earlier at home.

What closes that window isn’t lack of desire. Research by Nicholas Epley at the University of Chicago finds that people systematically underestimate how much strangers want to connect and overestimate how awkward the attempt will be. Both the newcomer and the potential neighbor hold back, each assuming the other isn’t interested. Both tend to be wrong.

And the effects of getting it right last longer than most people expect. Vanessa Elias, the founder of Block Party USA, shared with us the story of a neighbor who brought her family a pie when she moved in as a six year old. She remembers her name, Mrs. Fisher, decades later.

What would happen if welcoming became a civic practice instead of an individual virtue? This year, the US Chamber of Connection recruited over 250 people across 25 states to run creative experiments that aimed to help others feel more welcome in their neighborhoods. They were trained on the science of connection and worked together across the country to support each other. We call them the Welcome Committee.

For many of the volunteers, they signed up because they had an experience like Liz. One shared that “when we arrived to our new town we felt welcomed, surprised, and alive through these touches, and wanted to build more.” Or as another volunteer explained, “people are still welcoming me and I still feel at home here. So it gave me this strong sense of duty to pay that forward. I wanna create spaces where people can land and feel comfortable.”

For others, it came from an experience more akin to Darilys’s. “I would have loved to find this kind of thing when I moved,” shared one early volunteer. “I’ve been in my neighborhood and city since 2019 and have yet to make close friends in the neighborhood or city where I live. It feels scary and uncomfortable to get out there. But I’m realizing the huge impact of not having close friends. I’m ready to make the effort. Because if I’m feeling this way, I can’t be the only one.”

They each began by inviting a neighbor for a conversation. They shared their stories of their neighborhoods growing up. Dreamed about the ideal neighborhood together. Explored the Six Points of Connection, a clear and actionable checklist for individuals and leaders to define and strengthen social bonds. And then they explored how they might together make the neighborhood more connected.

They ran some creative experiments. Lindsay turned their suburban garage door into a community message board. Kaitlin created a traveling tin of homemade baked goods that moves neighbor to neighbor down the street, each recipient required to bake something and pass it on to someone new. Sarah hosted a gathering for other people named Sara(h) in her town. Courtney started flyering the neighborhood to organize a dog gathering in someone’s backyard. Nithya left a flower and a handwritten note for each of the five nearest neighbors inviting them to coffee.

Many of the volunteers nostalgically tried things they remembered from their childhood or had seen in a movie. Kate moved an iron chair and table to her front step and started having her morning coffee there facing the street. Jon made lemonade with his kids and set out donuts for anyone walking by. Chet started introducing himself on his daily dog walks. Anne organized a multi-household yard sale across eight houses. And many of them hosted potlucks and block parties.

They all expressed knowing “how meaningful it can be to walk into a new space and find someone who is genuinely glad you are there.” And they wanted to make people feel that way.

As the volunteers conducted their experiments, they began to share their findings. They mirrored much of what we have learned from social scientists over the last 50 years. Four themes emerged that help to inform how we can all do more to help new residents, and any neighbor, feel more welcomed.

1. The first step is the most important. Stop playing “invite chicken.” Many of our volunteers shared a version of the same experience: sitting at home hoping someone would call and invite them to hang out, attend an event, or even just watch Netflix together. They assumed that the people they wanted to call were probably busy or uninterested. Those people were likely sitting at home thinking the same thing. Nobody called. Everyone stayed alone.

Call it invite chicken: a standoff where everyone loses despite everyone wanting the same thing. The barrier isn’t reality; it’s anticipation. Someone just has to go first.

And sometimes, when we do break the game of chicken, we don’t get the response we wanted. When Hazel hosted her first neighborhood coffee gathering, one neighbor texted that he was too nervous to come. She didn’t give up. She texted back encouraging him. He came. At one of Elizabeth’s gatherings in San Diego, a man had been dragged along by his wife, who promised they’d stay just five minutes. He was the last to leave.

We are poor forecasters of our own social experiences. People overweight the discomfort of initiation and underweight the pleasure of connection. The host who sends the follow-up text, the wife who insists on five minutes, isn’t being pushy. They’re correcting for a cognitive error the reluctant person can’t see from the inside.

2. We are more motivated working together. Partner with someone. Erin hosted a neighborhood gathering in New York City and reflected afterward on what she’d do differently. Her top lesson: “I appreciated the help I did get from neighbors and wished I’d had a co-planner to share more of the work with.”

This is consistent with what research finds: People are significantly more likely to initiate and sustain kind and helpful behavior when they’re doing it alongside someone else. A partner doesn’t just make the work easier; they make follow-through more likely, because the social cost of backing out now involves someone else. Several Welcome Committee volunteers named the US Chamber of Connection cohort itself as the thing that got them out the door. Not the training, not the tools, but the fact that others were doing it, too.

3. We gain comfort with repeat exposure. Build a rhythm. Hansen has been hosting a monthly porch gathering (coffee, bagels, same spot) for two years. Recently, he watched neighbors spontaneously make plans to go cycling together and quietly organize to look out for a boy on the street whose father is dying of cancer. Neither of those things was planned. They happened because the same people had been attending his events and they had built comfort with each other. Hansen didn’t suggest they help the boy but had created the environment for it to be possible.

Research finds that repeated contact with the same faces makes us like and trust them more, independent of what’s said. Those repeated encounters build weak ties, which are how people find help in a crisis, how trust spreads beyond close friends, how a community starts to look out for itself. The first gathering gets you in the door. Showing up again is what builds the neighborhood.

What these moments share is spontaneity and clarity of intent. Research finds that unexpected gestures create a positive disruption that formal invitations don’t. They bypass the social calculation people run when a stranger approaches, and they signal warmth without asking for anything back. Research finds that being noticed is the precondition for feeling like you belong.

And having a clear role removes the ambiguity that makes first moves feel risky. When Annie said “I’m a Welcome Committee member,” her motives were transparent before she said another word. You don’t need a plan. You need to look up.

Giving to others produces more sustained well-being than spending on yourself. The helper’s high is real. Welcoming a newcomer isn’t just a form of service; it’s one of the highest-return social investments you can make.

Liz knows this firsthand. The woman who arrived in the Bay Area not knowing a soul, wondering if anyone would notice if she disappeared, is now the person who creates spaces for others to land. “People here took a chance on me,” she says. “So it gave me this strong sense of duty to pay that forward. I want to create spaces where people can land and feel comfortable.”

All friendships begin the same way: by talking to strangers. Someone just has to go first. Years later, Liz became the person who notices.

Aaron Hurst is a pioneer social entrepreneur and CEO focused on rebuilding human connection to strengthen communities. He founded the U.S. Chamber of Connection to address the national decline in social ties and trust. Previously, he founded the Taproot Foundation, sparking a $15B pro bono service movement, and led Imperative, which developed purpose profiling tools used by over 200 companies. He’s also co-founder of Board.Dev, connecting tech leaders to nonprofit boards. Author of The Purpose Economy, Aaron’s work has shaped the purpose movement in business and been featured in The New York Times, Bloomberg, and SSIR. He lives in Seattle with his family.

#135. Sources: Man City points deduction bombshell as 'likely' Premier League punishment revealed

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Founder & Managing Director AUTHORITY Former chief correspondent at Evening Standard, Goal and Press Association with nearly three decades in national, regional and digital news and sports journalism. Wayne directs the network’s news strategy and high-level operations. FOCUS Editorial direction for the 11-site network and newsroom management. THE BRIEF Wayne oversees the network’s editorial standards across the network. He provides the final sign-off on all headline scoops, ensuring content across all platforms meets the standards required for a high-velocity newsroom.

Man City are increasingly likely to get a single-season points deduction of around 40 to 50 points should their appeal against breaking Premier League financial rules fail, sources have told Football Insider.

The Citizens recently appealed the verdict that sent English football into a frenzy after they were found guilty of 114 charges relating to breaching Premier League financial rules between 2009 and 2018.

Sources say the Premier League are “very keen” to get the appeal wrapped up months before the 2026-27 campaign ends, but lawyer availability could be the biggest hurdle that prevents it from happening.

Should City's guilty verdict on most of the charges remain, then a separate hearing would determine sanctions and, behind the scenes, work is going on to determine what those would be.

Widespread calls for multiple seasons of points deductions are set to fall flat, as sources at the FA and Premier League suggest the greater likelihood is a points deduction that would give City a chance of staying up this season.

Such a sanction would be similar, in theory, to the punishment that Everton faced when they were deducted 10 points midway through the 2023-24 campaign but given enough time to potentially avoid relegation, which they did.

City are also likely to be hit with an extended transfer ban and an astronomical fine, which would keep them less competitive than usual for several seasons.

City have continued to deny any wrongdoing despite being found guilty of 114 counts of financial breaches over a nine-year period.

Recent reports have revealed that the Premier League has strengthened its legal team amid the ongoing battle with City, with leading law firm Slaughter and May and prominent barrister Sir James Eadie KC brought in to work alongside Bird & Bird as their case against the Citizens continues.

City's top-flight rivals are concerned that the case will not be settled this season, according to the Telegraph, which would bring huge uncertainty to the end of the campaign as they compete for the league title.

The club are ready to explore every avenue to overturn the verdict, with an appeal hearing date yet to be set while an independent panel is appointed to take in all the evidence.

Maresca's side are going from strength to strength this season as the charges linger over the club in the background.

The Italian has been forced to deal with the situation just months into his tenure as their new boss, but has remained certain in his belief that the club will come out unscathed.

Speaking to the media earlier in October, he said: "I joined the club because I completely trust, 100 per cent, the owner, the CEO, the sporting director, all the organisation. I don't have any doubts about that. I spoke with the club for sure. Probably the right moment to speak about that is when we're going to win this appeal.

"We completely understand the seriousness of the situation. I'm not saying we don't understand that. We understand, the players understand, but it's our duty, our job to try to control the noise. The managers and the players are focused on winning games, not about something different."

#13. ‘Really Hard To Believe,’ ‘I Didn’t See a Reason’ — Former ESPN Star Surprised by Pat McAfee Being ‘Bothered’ by Video From Utah Students

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This past weekend, the "College GameDay" crew traveled to the University of Utah in Salt Lake City ahead of the Utes' matchup with Houston.

While the event seemed to go off without a hitch, Pat McAfee later made clear that he did not enjoy his experience at Utah. It all happened before the show began, when McAfee saw Utah's official social media account post a video of students calling him "creepy" and another calling him "corny."

McAfee said he wouldn't have been bothered if the students had said these things on their personal pages; however, he found it distasteful that the school's official account posted the students' comments.

Former ESPN employee Linda Cohn spoke on her podcast, "The Linda Cohn Show," about the McAfee/Utah situation. She was surprised that McAfee had a problem with this video.

"My reaction was that's what caused all of this?… I didn't see a reason to turn the entire visit into a grievance. It's really hard to believe," Cohn said.

Even though McAfee wasn't happy with how the day began, Cohn still thinks he could have done better during the show to avoid alienating everyone who came to Utah to see it.

PREDICT THE CFB SEASON: PFN's Free CFB Playoff Predictor

Cohn continued, "You could have laughed at it; you could have given a little back to them; you're an entertainer; there's material sitting right there you could have made fun. Instead, by the end of Gameday, you're picking Houston; you're telling the Utah crowd, 'I hope I never see you guys again.'"

The university subsequently apologized for publishing the video, accepting responsibility for the decision and emphasizing that McAfee and the "College GameDay" crew remained welcome on campus. ESPN also issued a statement supporting McAfee and acknowledging his concerns about safety and security.

The former NFL punter has helped reshape the company's sports-media identity with his freewheeling approach, outspoken opinions and willingness to use language that traditional studio shows typically avoid.

When "The Pat McAfee Show" debuted on ESPN in September 2023, it came with a disclaimer that made that difference explicit.

"The opinions expressed on this show do not necessarily reflect the beliefs of their peers, their boss, or ESPN," the disclaimer read.

DIVE IN: PFN's Free CFB HQ — Team and Player Stats, Info, and More

It also warned viewers that there might be some "'cuss' words" because "that's how humans in the real world talk." The tongue-in-cheek message became part of the show's identity with its deliberately informal style.

McAfee remains a valuable part of ESPN's programming, and there is no indication that the Utah controversy will change his role at the network. Expect to keep seeing him on "College GameDay" unless they head back to Utah at some point, where he has made it clear he is unlikely to return.

#112. Bradford City boss Michael Duff on Huddersfield Town reunion plus praise for 'devastating' Terriers

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Duff managed Town for almost 10 months before being axed in March 2025, with many observers viewing his dismissal as harsh.

The 48-year-old is quick to stress that he has moved on following the events of that early spring as he prepares to face Town, who arrive at Valley Parade as league leaders with City residing in fourth spot.

Duff said: "I enjoyed the year that I was there; not quite a year. I've got nothing bad to say, but this weekend is not about me.

"It's two historic clubs with over 100 years of history, two big football clubs. That's what the day should be about.

"Two sets of supporters going at it, two players going at it. Hopefully a ding-dong game with a good football match.

"I want Bradford City to win, because I'm now at Bradford City and that's my job. I've got no ill feeling, it is what it is.

"Lots of things happen in football for a reason. Whether you think they're unjust or not. Everyone's got a story. No one's interested really."

Duff, whose side have not conceded a goal so far under his watch - with the run including four league matches - is conscious that his side will have to be at their best defensively against a Terriers side who are chasing their fourth straight league success.

He added: "They are a devastating counter-attacking team.

"They are quite happy to give the ball up at times, but they're doing that for a reason, because they are devastating on the turnover with Bali Mumba, Joe Taylor, (Derensili Sanches) Fernandes and (Ilias) Bronkhorst.

"I left there 18 months ago and I think there's two players that are left in the squad, so it shows the turnover they've had. They probably needed it but Martin (Drury) has done a really good job."

Duff admits to a real ‘quandary’ regarding who will replace suspended midfielder Callum Connolly.

Striker Stephen Humphreys (back) is set to miss out again, while Nick Powell (tonsilitis) is being assessed.

#118. Adam Phillips' 'relief' at breaking his Bradford City duck

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Midfielder Phillips was brought to Valley Parade from Barnsley in the summer precisely for his ability to regularly find the net at League One level. He did it 37 times in 156 appearances for the Reds.

But, hampered by injury, he never hit his stride under previous manager Graham Alexander.

Did you know with a Digital subscription to Yorkshire Post, you can get access to all of our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

The arrival of Michal Duff, who he worked so well with at Oakwell in 2022-23, has seen Phillips become more prominent and he has got into good goalscoring positions without scoring until Tuesday's night's 2-0 League Cup last-16 win over Peterborough United when, after twice letting himself down with a heavy touch when through one-on-one, he beat Alex Bass,

"It's probably about 95 per cent relief, I think," he commented.

"I came to this club as a goalscorer and to get chances and create chances, get assists.

“I've been a little bit unlucky. I hit the crossbar home against Leyton Orient, had one cleared off the line against Stevenage and then the one against Burton was probably my own fault, lacking a bit of confidence.

"Just to get off the mark was a massive relief and I'm really happy to contribute to the team and get us into the next round.

“I've never really experienced it in my career to have that many chances and nothing falls.

"I had two chances before and I'm just thinking, 'Is it going to come?’

“After the second one, I just wanted the ground to swallow me up. But credit to myself to keep going, get another chance and I was happy to take it.”

The 28-year-old admitted his transfer had not yet gone as he wanted.

“I'm not making excuses but I had a couple of injuries in pre-season so there was a little bit of a setback," he explained. "I was a little bit behind on fitness and I've been in and out of the team.

“To get a goal and hopefully a run of games with lots coming up now will do me a massive favour.

“When you come into a new club, you're learning a new style of play.

"I know the manager's changed again, but there's not a lot changed.

“For it to be a little bit stop-start is tough but I've just got to get my head down now, work as hard as I can and build on this."

#305. Bitwise Raises Bitcoin 2036 Target to $1.6M as BTC Hits $81K

📅 Oct 29, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Bitwise forecasts Bitcoin's price will have attained $1.6 million by 2036, with a bull case of $3.7 million and a bear case of $131,000.

The biggest risks for BTC are quantum computing, AI-fueled mathematical advances, and regulatory evolution.

The crypto community has criticized their method for offering a wide range of possibilities.

Bitwise Asset Management has raised its 10-year base-case price target for Bitcoin (BTC) from $1.3 million to $1.6 million. The revision is driven by institutional rotation into the digital asset, expanding BTC markets and stabilizing market cycles.

Dubbed the “2027 Bitcoin Long-Term Capital Market Assumptions,” the report discussed Bitcoin’s volatility, returns, and correlation with other assets in the next decade.

To begin with, Bitwise notes several addressable markets for Bitcoin. Institutions top the list, followed by emerging market currencies, store of value, and offshore wealth.

Currently, global institutions control about $140 trillion in assets – an amount Bitwise projects could grow to $304 trillion by 2035. A 1%-5% allocation from this pool would generate $1.5 trillion to $7.4 trillion in Bitcoin purchases.

Factoring in this demand, Bitwise then calculates possible Bitcoin prices for 2036, starting at a September price of $83,557. Its base case is $1,599,766, bull case is $3,667,911, and bear case is $130,686.

Note that the math also factors in Bitcoin’s compound annual growth rate of 34.3%, an average volatility of 27.8%, and average correlation to US stocks at 0.43.

In the end, Bitwise concludes that it is confident Bitcoin will be the best-performing asset of the next decade.

While the best-case scenario gives much to hope for, Bitwise warns against three major risks that could severely impact the crypto ecosystem.

The first is quantum computing developments, which could exploit vulnerabilities in Bitcoin’s public-key cryptography. Just recently, Google marked another achievement in this field, bringing us some time closer to Q-day.

The second is AI-driven mathematical advances, which could completely bypass quantum computing to break Bitcoin’s cryptography using existing technology.

Last but not least are regulatory reversals, which could massively impact institutional investor sentiment since their operations are bound by regulatory compliance.

Additionally, Bitwise cautions that volatility will persist in the face of ”significant but diminishing drawdowns.” A less conservative Monte Carlo simulation showed Bitcoin could achieve prices of over $4 million by year-end 2033, and $5.6 million by year-end 2034.

As Bitcoin crossed over to $81,000, the Bitwise report increased confidence in Bitcoin’s long-term value. Many, however, expressed dissatisfaction with the wide margin of predictions, saying it offers little certainty.

This is basically saying nothing at this point. “Our range of possibilities is basically everything”

#108. Carabao Cup quarter-final draw as Liverpool avoid Manchester City

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Holders Manchester City will face Bradford City in the quarter-finals of the EFL Cup after a penalty shootout win over Brighton.

Man City twice trailed in-form Brighton but Vitor Reis’ 72nd-minute equaliser took the last-16 tie at the Etihad Stadium to penalties.

Stand-in goalkeeper Geronimo Rulli emerged as City’s hero, saving two spot-kicks to send Enzo Maresca’s side into the quarters, where they will face Bradford.

Bradford beat fellow League One side Peterborough United 2-0 earlier in the week to reach the Carabao Cup quarter-finals for the first time since 2013.

Sign up to our daily football newsletter, pick your team and get the latest news sent directly to your inbox every morning.

Liverpool were also involved in a penalty shootout against Chelsea after Cody Gakpo and Morgan Rogers both scored from the spot in normal time.

Kostas Tsimikas and Emmanuel Emegha both missed chances to settle the shootout before Chelsea defender Wesley Fofana skied over with the decisive penalty.

Remarkably, it was Liverpool’s fifth successive win on penalties over Chelsea and meant Xabi Alonso and Jordan Henderson tasted defeat on their returns to Anfield.

Fleetwood Town vs Everton/Newcastle United

Liverpool will face Andoni Iraola’s former club Bournemouth for a place in the semi-finals after the Cherries put three goals past Aston Villa.

Elsewhere, Fleetwood Town’s reward for an incredible win over Premier League champions Arsenal is another top-flight opponent, with either Everton or Newcastle United travelling to Highbury Stadium.

#421. This map shows you which NYC restaurants are owned by private equity

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

For many consumers, “private equity” has become a four-letter word. Private equity firms have acquired the unpopular reputation of purchasing food establishments that then notoriously decline in quality, as new owners cut costs in search of new avenues to profit. They’ve also been criticized for displacing mom-and-pop shops in the process.

But beyond noticing the stereotypical signs of private equity backing—sudden spikes in new franchise locations and sleek, minimalist branding, to name two—consumers are often left guessing at a business’s ownership. That’s especially true in metropolises like New York City, where grabbing a bite means choosing from a whopping 31,000-plus food establishments. 

That’s where a trending new website steps in. On isitownedbyprivateequity.com, users can find the answers to the site’s titular question. Via an interactive map of New York City, you can zoom in on any bar, restaurant, or café and learn whether it’s owned or backed by private equity or venture capital, and whether it’s public or privately owned.

How the site works

The site was created by Dave Liebowitz, known on social media as DeFi Dave, who got the idea from a common complaint he’d seen on the internet.

“What initially inspired me to create it was walking around Manhattan on the weekends and seeing all the lines for different restaurants,” Liebowitz tells Fast Company via social media. “Then I would go online and see the discourse around private equity blaming them for the lines and decrease in quality.”

He went on to create the site with Grok Bot and Cursor in a single day (an experience he described as “psychedelic” in a follow-up post), then shared it on social media. Liebowitz’s X post announcing the site has since garnered more than 178,000 views.

So far, the site has amassed info on more than 5,000 NYC eateries. And it’s growing fast, with 1,320 of those listings being added in the day since the site’s launch.

Of the currently tracked restaurants, bars, and cafés, 28% are owned or backed by private equity, with firm Roark Capital alone accounting for 842 of them. (Nearly all of those are Dunkin’ Donuts and Subway locations—Roark acquired Dunkin’ in 2020 and Subway in 2024.)

Clicking on a business opens a window with tons of info, including its ownership status, nearby privately owned alternatives, and a scorecard for its neighborhood showing what share of food spots in that area are owned or backed by private equity. According to the site, that data all comes from investor and parent-company portfolio pages, as well as from Wikipedia articles that cite primary sources.

The story beyond private equity

Liebowitz emphasizes that being a private or family-owned business doesn’t automatically correlate with quality.

In another post on X, he took aim at Black Seed Bagels—a chain with sleek, clean branding that fits right in with the private equity crowd, but is actually privately owned. According to Liebowitz, that didn’t stop the bagel shop from skimping on the lox on his sandwich, much to his chagrin.

His map accounts for stereotype-defying establishments with its “before vs. after the deal” window. For private equity-owned or -backed businesses with enough data, the site shows how their quality has changed over time, based on their American Customer Satisfaction Index (ACSI) scores.

For example, Panera Bread has dropped by 4 points on the ACSI since its 2017 acquisition by JAB Holding Co., while Subway gained 5 points since being bought by Roark.

Rather than passing hard judgment on “good” or “bad” places to eat, Liebowitz says he hopes his site can simply help consumers be better informed about where their money is going.

“I believe more transparency is always good, especially when it comes to where people decide to spend their money and what businesses to support,” he says. “In addition, I hope people use the site to be more objective about restaurants they patronize.”

Looking ahead, Liebowitz plans to expand the site to include San Francisco and Los Angeles establishments, as well as to let users submit their own information about businesses to build on the site’s existing data. He also hopes to create maps for industries other than food service that are often targeted by private equity firms, including nursing homes, real estate, healthcare, and dentistry.

#323. Manhunt underway for violent illegal immigrant who was released under NYC sanctuary policies

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

DHS Secretary Markwayne Mullin joins Bill Hemmer to discuss a new House GOP report on New York City's sanctuary policies alleging officials downgraded charges to avoid deporting illegal immigrants.

FIRST ON FOX: New York City's sanctuary policy is again coming under fire after authorities released an illegal immigrant accused of assaulting a police officer with a deadly weapon without notifying or cooperating with federal immigration authorities.

The Department of Homeland Security (DHS) sent repeated requests for federal officials to detain Alpha Bah, an illegal immigrant from Guinea, after he was arrested on charges of aggravated assault on a police officer with a deadly weapon, resisting arrest, obstructing governmental administration and intent to obtain transportation without paying, Fox News Digital has learned.

Immigration and Customs Enforcement (ICE) lodged a detainer request with Queens Central Booking on Oct. 7, 2026. The New York City Police Department (NYPD) ignored the detainer and released Bah without notifying ICE.

"This is the vision of sanctuary politicians in a nutshell: the open-border policies of the Biden Administration let this criminal into our country, and then sanctuary politicians in New York released him after arresting him without notifying ICE," DHS Secretary Markwayne Mullin told Fox News Digital.

Bah's release is just one of many examples of sanctuary cities in the U.S. allowing illegal immigrants back into the U.S. following their arrest, and it flies in the face of the Trump administration's crackdown on allowing people without legal status to remain in the country.

HOUSE REPORT SAYS NYC PROSECUTORS TAILORED PLEA DEALS TO SHIELD ILLEGAL IMMIGRANTS FROM DEPORTATION

The Department of Homeland Security is calling on sanctuary politicians to cooperate with Immigration and Customs Enforcement amid the repeated release of an illegal immigrant, Alpha Bah, who remains at large. (DHS/ICE)

"This illegal alien from Guinea was arrested for assaulting a police officer with a deadly weapon. Despite these violent charges, New York City sanctuary politicians refused to honor ICE’s detainer and RELEASED him back onto the streets, and he remains at large," Mullin said in a statement.

He added, "DHS is calling on Governor Kathy Hochul and Mayor Zohran Mamdani to knock it off with these evil sanctuary policies before another American gets hurt, or worse."

ICE has now twice requested that Bah be held in custody. He was previously charged with intent to obtain transportation without paying in June 2026. ICE lodged a detainer with Manhattan Central Booking, but the NYPD also released him following that arrest.

Bah entered the United States illegally in November 2023. He resided in Arizona before being arrested by the U.S. Border Patrol.

He was also arrested in January 2025 for criminal trespassing but was released before ICE was able to request a detainer on him under Biden-era policies, according to DHS.

HOCHUL REMAINS DEFIANT AFTER SHERIFFS SUE OVER LAW BANNING COOPERATION WITH ICE: 'BRING IT ON'

New York Gov. Kathy Hochul listens as New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani speaks during the annual New York State Financial Control Board meeting on Aug. 12, 2026, in New York City. (Michael M. Santiago/Getty Images)

Bah’s release has prompted DHS to call on all pro-sanctuary politicians, not just Hochul and Mamdani, to reconsider policies that restrict cooperation with federal immigration authorities.

Gov. Kathy Hochul, D-N.Y., has been a large proponent of implementing policies that place restrictions on state and local law enforcement cooperating with federal immigration authorities. In May, she signed legislation that imposed laws blocking New York's law enforcement agencies from entering into official agreements with ICE.

New York City’s socialist Mayor Zohran Mamdani has similarly supported sanctuary policies in the city, signing an executive order to "reaffirm the city’s commitment to being a sanctuary for all New Yorkers."

The order made it more difficult to access data of illegal immigrants, restricted federal immigration enforcement on city property and put in place an audit to check that city agencies are complying with sanctuary policies.

MAMDANI MESSAGE TO RESIDENTS ON HOW TO DEAL WITH ICE RESULTS IN VIRAL INTERNET BACKLASH: 'TREASON'

Homeland Security Secretary Markwayne Mullin speaks during a press conference at the U.S. Citizenship and Immigration Services (USCIS) New York field office on Sept. 1, 2026, in New York City, New York. (Michael M. Santiago/Getty Images)

"The U.S. Immigration and Customs Enforcement (ICE) partnerships with local law enforcement are key to removing criminal illegal aliens including murderers, rapists, pedophiles, gang members, and terrorists from American communities," DHS said in a statement responding to the introduced policies. "7 of the 10 safest cities in the U.S. cooperate with ICE."

Neither Hochul's nor Mamdani's offices immediately replied to Fox News Digital's request for comment.

#397. Apple Unveils Its First-Ever MacBook Pro With a Touchscreen OLED

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Apple Unveils Its First-Ever MacBook Pro With a Touchscreen OLED

Apple has announced its first-ever touchscreen-equipped Macs. The redesigned MacBook Pro models are also Apple’s first laptops with OLED displays. It’s a big day for the Mac.

The MacBook Pro has always been controlled through its trackpad and keyboard. Apple has never made a touchscreen-equipped Mac before. While the latest iPad Pro and iPadOS 27 bring Apple’s tablet closer than ever to a Mac-like experience, a gap has long existed between the Mac and touchscreens. The gap has now officially closed.

“The new MacBook Pro with PROLED XDR Display is a monumental step forward for the Mac,” says Johny Srouji, Apple’s chief hardware officer.

“PROLED XDR is the world’s best laptop display. It pairs state-of-the-art tandem OLED technology with advanced algorithms in our display engine to deliver the kind of visual fidelity pros have been dreaming of. And with the magic of Touch coming to MacBook Pro, combined with the game-changing performance of M5 Pro and M5 Max, we’ve created a whole new way for pros to interact with their content, taking their workflows to a completely new level. The new MacBook Pro is the result of the deep integration between Apple silicon, hardware, and software, and there’s simply nothing else like it,” Srouji continues.

At a “Pro and Tell” event in Los Angeles today, Apple revealed arguably the most significant MacBook Pro update ever, and certainly the biggest change to the popular notebook series since Apple moved to its own Apple Silicon M-series chips in 2020. The new MacBook Pro, available in 14- and 16-inch models, marks a major shift for the MacBook Pro, which has largely kept the same form factor and functionality since the first MacBook Pro arrived 20 years ago.

At first glance, the redesigned MacBook Pro looks familiar. It also packs the familiar M5 Pro and M5 Max chips inside, which, as PetaPixel has seen, are incredible. However, that’s about where the similarities end.

The new MacBook Pro sports what Apple calls a “PROLED XDR” display, essentially an OLED “XDR”. It is a tandem OLED design with 2,000 nits of peak brightness, a 120Hz refresh rate, and the exceptionally black blacks that OLEDs are known for. Apple promises that the screen is effectively a professional-grade, portable reference monitor for creatives.

With the move to the new OLED touchscreen, the illuminated Mac logo on the back has returned, too.

Apple describes the PROLED XDR as “the world’s best display in a pro laptop.” The company specifically cites its effectiveness for photographers, colorists, and content creators. The super-wide color gamut supports P3 and now Adobe RGB workflows, and Apple promises better coverage in the green and cyan ranges in particular. It also offers more than 80% Rec. 2020 coverage for HDR video editing and color grading, according to Apple.

With up to 2,000 nits of peak SDR brightness, the new MacBook Pro should perform well outdoors. Those who require anti-glare performance can also opt for a nano-texture display. The screen also includes a Dynamic Island, like the iPhone, instead of the traditional notch. This serves as the new home for Spotlight, Live Activities, and Siri AI.

The bezels are also thinner, which means slightly larger screens without increasing the footprint. The 16-inch display is now 16.3 inches, while the 14-inch one is 14.2 inches diagonally.

Beyond the bright, sharp, high-contrast, and colorful display, it’s also obviously notable that it can be touched. Apple says this makes everyday interactions easier and more intuitive, like scrolling, swiping, and pinching elements on screen. Apple builds the touch sensor directly into the display panel, rather than layering it on top, which it says reduces latency.

“Touch also introduces entirely new ways of working for pro users. Graphic designers can now use the entire display as their canvas, no longer constrained by the area of the trackpad when drawing; musicians can play software instruments right onscreen; and app developers can test how Touch performs in their iOS and iPadOS apps directly on the MacBook Pro display,” Apple says. The MacBook Pro’s new touchscreen also works alongside existing keyboard and trackpad shortcuts.

Apple also says the newly designed hinge is stronger than before, so the screen shouldn’t wobble when you touch it. Apple also developed new video stabilization to keep FaceTime calls from getting shaky when the caller touches their screen.

Apple’s first-party apps are optimized for the new MacBook Pro straight out of the gate, and macOS automatically translates taps into clicks. However, third-party developers will want to use new touch APIs to make sure their apps take full advantage of the new MacBook Pro. Some apps are already optimized, though, including DaVinci Resolve and Adobe Lightroom.

The new MacBook Pro is also thinner and lighter. The 14-inch MacBook Pro now weighs half a pound less, tipping the scales at just three pounds. The 16-inch version is four pounds, 0.7 pounds lighter than before. Both models are slimmer thanks to the OLED’s thinner stack. It’s not a huge difference, though: the 14-inch is half a millimeter thinner, and the 16-inch is nearly a full millimeter slimmer.

Audio matters, too. An all-new woofer design promises 50% deeper bass, and new tweeters that Apple says improve sound clarity. For the first time in a MacBook Pro, Apple also added new audio tuning specifically for closed-lid use. Apple says the closed laptop will still deliver great audio, replacing the need for dedicated speakers when using external displays.

The M5 Pro and M5 Max chips inside are the same as prior Macs. That is, they are very powerful.

“It raises the bar for what was already the world’s best pro laptop. We love the Mac, and it’s the reason why so many of us found our way to Apple in the first place. The Mac has never been better, and it’s never been stronger,” Apple’s new CEO, John Ternus, says.

The new MacBook Pro has three Thunderbolt 5 ports, HDMI, and an SDXC card slot. The laptop supports up to three external displays with M5 Pro and up to four with M5 Max, plus the PROLED XDR.

The laptop includes Wi-Fi 7 and Bluetooth 6 connectivity. It can fast charge up to 50% in just 30 minutes and promises up to 24 hours of battery life.

The MacBook Pro also offers several other interesting features with PROLED XDR. A new Apple Pro Display Calibrator lets users calibrate the built-in display to very specific targets. A new Apple Color Matching Function 2026 lets the PROLED XDR perfectly match an iPhone 18 Pro or Studio Display XDR.

The new MacBook Pro starts at $2,899 for the 14-inch model with M5 Pro and $3,399 for the 16-inch one with M5 Pro. The M5 Pro models start with 24GB of unified memory and expand to 64GB. Storage starts at 1TB, expandable to 4TB.

They are also available with the M5 Max. The 14-inch starts at $4,449, and the M5 Max starts at $4,799. These start with an 18-core CPU and a 32-core GPU. An upgraded 40-core GPU is also available for $300. These machines start with 36GB of unified memory and 2TB SSDs, and can be expanded to 128GB of RAM and, for the first time, 16TB of internal storage.

For reference, a maxed-out 16-inch MacBook Pro with 128GB of unified memory and 16TB of storage is $14,549. A nano-texture screen is an extra $150.

The new MacBook Pro is available to preorder now and releases on November 6.

The Witchbrook release date announcement kicked things off for the presentation. While definitely exciting, that's not what enthralled me the most.

#325. GOP senator takes aim at blue-city mayor for move promoting foreign workers in key industry: 'Lit on fire'

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Equal Employment Opportunity Commission Chair Andrea Lucas discusses Instacart's $5 million settlement with the Department of Justice and EEOC over allegations of favoring temporary visa holders over U.S. citizens. Lucas warns other employers about the legal consequences of prioritizing foreign workers over domestic applicants.

FIRST ON FOX: Sen. Bernie Moreno (R-OH) is taking aim at the Democratic mayor of Columbus, Ohio, over a memorandum of understanding (MOU) that was signed with the African nation of Ghana promoting a joint nursing program.

"Instead of investing in Ohio’s domestic workforce, you and the Columbus City Council chose to direct $225,000 in public dollars towards recruiting nurses from overseas," Moreno wrote to Mayor Andrew Ginther on Wednesday morning, expressing "disappointment" over the MOU in Columbus, the state capital of Ohio.

"But this figure is only a fraction of the approximately $8.3 million in taxpayer dollars you have lit on fire to benefit noncitizens and foreign governments since you were elected as mayor."

Moreno’s criticism comes after the city and Ghanaian officials signed a memorandum of understanding in September establishing the Ghana-Columbus Nursing Partnership Programme. The agreement is intended to strengthen nursing education, expand workforce development and create legal pathways for qualified Ghanaian nurses to enter Ohio’s healthcare workforce.

RED-STATE SENATOR DROPS HAMMER ON DEM MAYOR OVER NEW 'WOKE' DEI ORDINANCE WHILE VIOLENT CRIME SURGES

Ohio Republican Sen. Bernie Moreno repeatedly pressed a former Biden adviser on whether illegal immigration is a crime in a heated hearing. (Photo by Chip Somodevilla/Getty Images)

Moreno argued that the agreement could result in American nurses being replaced by lower-cost foreign labor and said public funds should instead be used to expand enrollment and training capacity at Ohio colleges and universities.

"You were elected to serve the citizens of Columbus, not the citizens of Ghana," Moreno wrote. "Increasing publicly subsidized foreign nursing recruitment programs will likely negatively impact American nurses’ wages. These funds should be invested directly in Ohio nursing students and utilized to expand Ohio’s universities and colleges nursing programs’ capacity. I recommend you implement policies that invest public funds in American nurses."

AMERICA’S BROKEN LEGAL IMMIGRATION SYSTEM IS REPLACING US WORKERS BY DESIGN

Mayor of Columbus, Ohio Andrew J. Ginther delivers remarks during the NCAA Football Championship celebration at Ohio Stadium on January 26, 2025, in Columbus, Ohio. (Jason Mowry/Getty Images)

The partnership between Ghana and Columbus has been widely blasted by conservatives on social media in recent weeks, with many arguing that American citizens should fill those jobs.

"Why aren’t our tax dollars funding nursing scholarships and careers for Ohioans instead?" Attorney and GOP strategist Mehek Cooke posted on X in response to reporting from journalist Natalie Winters. "Stop sidelining Americans with OUR money."

"It’s worth noting that earlier this year a homecare nurse from Ghana named Roberta Achampong fled the country back to Ghana after allegedly stealing millions from Medicaid and buying a swanky house and a Porsche," policy analyst Parker Thayer posted on X. "And now the mayor of Columbus wants more nurses from Ghana.'

The $225,000 referenced in Moreno’s letter was approved by Columbus City Council as a 2026 general fund grant to Greater Columbus Sister Cities International. The ordinance says the money is intended to support business, government, cultural, educational and environmental exchanges among Columbus’ 10 sister cities, including Accra, Ghana, as well as the organization’s staffing and administrative costs.

A spokesperson for Ginther's office said in a statement to Fox News Digital that it is "unfortunate" that Moreno is "fanning the flames of conspiracy theories instead of working with cities to create affordable housing and address residents’ everyday needs."

"As America’s opportunity city, we welcome neighbors who help grow our economy and build a stronger, more prosperous community," the statement said. "The initiative is designed to complement existing domestic workforce development efforts, not replace them, and to help address the need for more nurses to meet nursing shortages. Its implementation will remain employer-driven, standards-based, and consistent with applicable U.S. laws and professional requirements. It does not constitute a City-funded hiring commitment or guarantee employment to any participating nurse. No City of Columbus funding is being used or committed. Additionally, no funding from the 2026 Sister Cities grant is being used to support staff time, travel, events, or other implementation activities associated with this initiative."

"Ghana has made significant investment in training nursing professionals who can migrate legally to enter a ready market in Columbus," Ginther said during the signing ceremony, according to a press release from the government of Ghana.

The press release added that Ghanaian officials "emphasized that the agreement extends far beyond simple labor migration and stated that the MoU serves as a strategic platform for: mutual skills transfer between medical professionals, institutional strengthening for healthcare facilities, joint research collaborations and health innovations and global improvement of patient health outcomes."

Moreno wrote that he hopes the mayor will "reallocate public dollars toward expanding enrollment and training capacity at area colleges and universities, which would generate additional high-earning job opportunities for younger generations in our community and improve patient care."

#310. NYC office-to-apartment mega project resumes after near disaster

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Crews lifted sagging floors at the Midtown East Pfizer HQ conversion after a July structural failure tied to missing reinforcement. New York's conversion pipeline continues, supported by state law property tax incentives and new permits.  AI Summary

Crews have finished lifting the sagging floors at the former Pfizer headquarters, the largest office-to-apartment conversion in city history.

A July structural failure forced evacuations across seven Midtown East blocks and, for a fleeting moment, threw a menacing cloud over a nationwide push to turn empty offices into housing.

Two columns buckled at the 1,600-unit conversion, which touched off inspections citywide, and stop-work orders followed. Concern spread nationwide, generating questions over cost, safety and design in adaptive reuse of decades-old office buildings.

Cities from Washington to Los Angeles have bet that converting vacant offices can add housing faster than ground-up construction. New York leads that push, and others emulate its tax breaks and zoning changes.

Mayor Zohran Mamdani inherited a conversion pipeline of about 12,000 units and has embraced it as a pillar of his own housing affordability pitch. Mamdani has leaned into commercial-to-residential conversion as part of a multi-pronged approach to increasing housing supply.

More than 40 other conversions are underway or planned citywide, adding more than 14,000 homes. The city has issued 16 new building permits for conversions since July.

The pipeline is holding steady. State tax law’s 467-m program offers a property tax exemption of up to 35 years for rental conversions that include affordable units. A June 30 deadline this year locked in the tax break.

Nate Bliss, founder of Latent Urban Ventures, told HousingWire TBD that applications are picking back up.

“There’s a lot of activity of people trying to get their projects moving in the next six months, so they can meet the June 30, 2028, deadline” for a 30-year break for projects that start by mid-2028, Bliss said. “We still have a long way to go and plenty of office stock to convert.”

This past summer, two columns buckled on the 21st floor of the Pfizer building make-over, under floor area recently added to the top of the building. The project’s structural engineer blamed missing steel reinforcement, rather than design. Inspectors found no imminent structural dangers.

New York City Buildings Commissioner Ahmed Tigani said in a social media video that the tower “has remained stable” and poses no danger to the public. Developer engineers designed a plan to jack up floors above the affected area, which had dropped about six inches, Tigani said.

Senior department engineers and an independent third-party firm vetted the plan before approval.

“That plan has moved forward and is now complete,” Tigani said.

Some lower-floor work has been allowed to continue. Upper floors remain limited to housekeeping and repairs tied to the fix.

“We will not allow work to proceed unless we are confident that all could be done safely,” Tigani said.

MetroLoft and David Werner Real Estate Investments lead the project, which includes about 400 affordable, rent-stabilized apartments.

Inspections of 180 sites turned up 65 violations across 38 locations. They led to 18 partial stop-work orders and one full halt. Work has resumed at some of those sites.

Deals continue. In September, Capstone Equities and BH3 Management landed a $208 million loan through Northwind Group to convert upper floors of a three-year-old, never-occupied 24-story downtown Brooklyn tower into 239 apartments.

BH3’s Adam Falk told The Wall Street Journal the conversion should take 18 to 20 months instead of four to five years for new development.

President Donald Trump on Thursday signed an executive order to reduce housing costs that could save some mortgage borrowers thousands of dollars, but the final text is narrower than an earlier draft that had circulated in recent weeks.

eXp Realty and Newrez announced Revenos Mortgage, a joint venture set to launch in early 2027. The column argues that as home sales and mortgage volume stay muted, large firms can gain share through vertical integration, raising questions about whether savings reach consumers.

#422. AI costumes are seeping into your real-life Halloween. Here’s what to look out for

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Amid the vampires, zombies, and ghosts out and about this Halloween, a modern monster is entering the mix: artificial intelligence.

An Instagram user named Daria (@that.f.daria) went viral for her AI-generated costume concepts, including what she described as “the only Halloween costume that actually scares me”: A personified usage limit for Claude. A short AI-generated clip of her wearing the costume has garnered two million views on Instagram, with some commenters asking where they could get their own spiky orange ensemble.

View this post on Instagram

A post shared by DARIA F* (@that.f.daria)

Daria followed up that post with another AI-generated costume in the same vein, this time the ChatGPT logo with its own “limit reached” message pasted on the front.

View this post on Instagram

A post shared by DARIA F* (@that.f.daria)

While commenters on Instagram laughed at Daria’s costume concepts, they weren’t such a big hit on other platforms. When screenshots of Daria’s ChatGPT costume and of another creator’s AI-generated take on her Claude costume made their way to X, users said they were a sign of the end times. “Send the flood,” reads one simple response with more than 354,000 views.

“Literally cannot understand how much AI you have to be using to experience this and be upset about it,” another user wrote. “Are you just having none of your own thoughts at all?”

Night of the living AI

After going viral, Daria decided to make her Claude costume in real life. That entailed drafting a pattern in the shape of the Anthropic logo, then stuffing orange fabric with foam.

View this post on Instagram

A post shared by DARIA F* (@that.f.daria)

Though Daria’s results remain to be seen, she also posted about starting “a global trend,” sharing screenshots of several other accounts that had generated their own takes on her Claude costume, as well as one so-called “scammer” who’d apparently taken her photos and claimed to be selling the costume for $109.99.

“They’re using both screenshots of me AND AI-generated images of ‘me,’” Daria wrote in her caption. “I’m happy to inspire people. I’m less happy to become the unpaid face of someone else’s scam.”

Hungry for brains . . . or brainrot

While Daria is DIY-ing a real version of her Claude costume, other AI-inspired creations are already for sale in costume shops.

Spirit Halloween, for one, offers costumes based on AI-generated “Italian brainrot” characters, including Tung Tung Tung Sahur (an anthropomorphic wooden drum wielding a baseball bat) and Ballerina Cappuccina (a ballerina with—you guessed it—a cappuccino for a head).

Whether these costumes will actually prove popular is another question entirely, but Reddit users who identified themselves as Spirit Halloween employees confirmed that they’ve made at least some sales of the AI-inspired costumes. Retailing at $44.99, the Tung Tung Tung Sahur costume is sold out online in all but one size.

#309. Blue Origin leases former Bartell Drugs warehouse south of Seattle for new manufacturing site

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

by Kurt Schlosser on Oct 28, 2026 at 8:25 amOctober 28, 2026 at 12:33 pm

Blue Origin is taking over an industrial building in Des Moines, Wash., where the Jeff Bezos-founded space company plans a mix of lab, development and light production work, the company confirmed to GeekWire.

The 265,922-square-foot facility, known as Prologis Park SeaTac 3, was previously a Bartell Drugs distribution center just south of Seattle-Tacoma International Airport. Blue Origin said that at full capacity over the long term, the site is expected to support hundreds of employees.

KING 5 first reported the news Tuesday, citing a state environmental application filed in September that lists Blue Origin Manufacturing LLC at the building’s address, 21202 24th Ave. S. A pre-application under review by the city indicates the property could be used for research and development of commercial satellites, the station reported.

Commercial real estate firm Newmark announced a lease for the entire building last week without naming the tenant.

“The manufacturing project that will be using this property is cutting-edge and we are excited about the job growth and possibilities that it represents for our region,” Newmark Executive Vice Chairman Thad Mallory said in a news release.

With roughly 33,000 residents, the small city of Des Moines is becoming something of an aerospace hub.

Blue Origin’s new location is around the corner from where Shield AI is building X-Forge 3, a 420,000-square-foot facility that will make the company’s X-BAT autonomous fighter jet. Shield AI said last month it expects to have 4,000 workers in the region by the early 2030s, and that it’s looking for another 1 million square feet.

“It’s all about labor and access to power,” Mallory told KING 5, referring to the advanced manufacturing projects coming to the area.

Blue Origin is headquartered in nearby Kent, Wash., about 5 miles from the Des Moines site.

The company is working to get its New Glenn rocket flying again after one exploded during a ground engine test in May, which damaged its Florida launch pad. New Glenn is slated to carry Blue’s Blue Moon Mark 2 lander on NASA’s Artemis 3 mission, a test in Earth orbit planned for mid-2027 ahead of a crewed moon landing targeted for 2028.

#356. Alarming Attacks on Women Politicians

📅 Oct 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Click to expand Image Ana Elisa Santos Silva of the Workers' Party (PT), the youngest federal deputy elected in Minas Gerais state, poses for a photograph in Divinopolis, Brazil, on October 15, 2026. © 2026 Douglas Magno/AFP via Getty Images

Overwhelmed by threats—and fearing for her personal safety Satya Rhodes-Conway the current mayor of Madison, Wisconsin in the US, announced on October 23 that she was dropping her bid for reelection. "The price of being in public office has gotten too high,” she said.

Globally, women in public life face alarming levels of political violence, including tech-facilitated sexual and gender-based violence, with little protection or recourse. While research finds chillingly high rates of violence against both women and men in politics, women are at greater risk and face more sexualized and gendered violence.

After Australian MP Helen Haines was deliberately hit by a car, doctors had to amputate both of her legs. In South Africa, a bloody campaign season included the killing of party activist Jeanette Magugu and a brutal home invasion against mayoral candidate Nhongo Solo. In Brazil, Ana Elisa Santos Silva and other candidates for public office filed police complaints about death and sexual violence threats on the campaign trail; a city councilor, Iara Pimentel, closed her X account due to threats and was hospitalized after a brick hit her at a rally. Bernadette Greene-Placentia, a US congressional candidate in Arizona, reported that she was attacked while repairing campaign posters and raped.

While attackers’ motives are often unclear, the result is the same--women and girls who speak up for their communities increasingly experience gendered violence and threats. After a 15-year-old made a video crudely lambasting India’s Prime Minister, she was inundated with rape and death threats. An Iraqi women’s rights leader who challenged an abusive amendment to Iraq’s personal status law was shot dead outside her home.

Technological developments also fuel gendered abuses. Of 720 European Parliament members, 46 women and four men had been depicted in or seen their online presence linked to deep fake sexualized images, according to a 2026 study. In the Asia-Pacific region, 18 percent of participating women parliamentarians faced doxing, according to a study.

These crimes are taking place amid a global backlash against women’s rights, with political power for women declining in many countries. After years of progress, the percentage of women national cabinet members globally declined to 22 percent in 2026, from 23 percent in 2024.

Experts increasingly warn that political violence deters women from running for office. With growing evidence that women’s leadership brings economic and social benefits, we all lose when women are pushed out of politics.
